  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Manasa adalah dewi ular dalam Hindu. Ia dipuja untuk mencegah dan menyembuhkan gigitan ular serta memohon kesuburan dan kemakmuran.",
      "en": "Manasa is a Hindu snake goddess. She is worshipped for preventing and curing snakebite and for fertility and prosperity.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manasa-c01",
        "manasa-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Teks Hindu menyebutnya saudari Vasuki dan istri resi Jaratkaru. Astika disebut sebagai putranya. Versi Purana menyebut Manasa sebagai putri Kashyapa yang lahir dari pikiran. Koleksi Cleveland mencatat lukisan Manasa dewi ular dari Kalighat di Kolkata sekitar 1890.",
      "en": "Hindu texts call her Vasuki's sister and sage Jaratkaru's wife. Astika is called her son. The Puranic version calls Manasa Kashyapa's mind born daughter. The Cleveland collection records a Manasa snake goddess painting from Kalighat in Kolkata around 1890.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manasa-c03",
        "manasa-c04",

        "manasa-c06"
      ]
    }

      "context": "religious-tradition",

      "id": "nataraja-c04",
      "source_id": "nataraja-s1",
      "quote": "The word Nataraja is a Sanskrit term, from नट Nata meaning \"act, drama, dance\" and राज Raja meaning \"king, lord\"; it can be roughly translated as Lord of the dance or King of the dance.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Nataraja menggabungkan nata dan raja dalam arti Penguasa Tari.",
        "en": "The name Nataraja combines nata and raja meaning Lord of Dance."
      }

      "id": "nataraja-c05",
      "source_id": "nataraja-s1",
      "quote": "This form is present in most Shiva temples, and is the prime deity in the Nataraja Temple at Chidambaram (Tillai).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia merupakan dewa utama kuil Nataraja di Chidambaram.",
        "en": "He is the principal deity at the Nataraja Temple in Chidambaram."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nataraja-c06",
      "source_id": "nataraja-s2",
      "quote": "With every step in his dance, he lands on a dwarfish figure personifying ignorance.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Museum Cleveland menafsirkan sosok kerdil yang diinjak dalam tarian Shiva sebagai personifikasi ketidaktahuan.",

      "id": "Tridevi adalah trinitas dewi dalam Hindu yang biasanya mencakup Saraswati, Lakshmi, dan Parvati. Sebagian denominasi memandangnya sebagai versi perempuan Trimurti atau pasangan para dewa Trimurti.",
      "en": "Tridevi is a Hindu trinity of goddesses usually comprising Saraswati, Lakshmi, and Parvati. Some denominations regard it as a feminine Trimurti or the Trimurti gods' consorts.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tridevi-c01",
        "tridevi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Shaktisme ketiganya merupakan manifestasi Mula Prakriti atau Mahadevi. Dalam Shaktisme peran pencipta, pemelihara, dan penghancur diberikan kepada Mahasaraswati, Mahalakshmi, dan Mahakali. Saraswati adalah dewi pembelajaran, seni, dan musik serta pasangan Brahma. Mackenzie menuturkan versi Varaha Purana tentang satu dewi yang terbagi menjadi Saraswati, Lakshmi, dan Parvati.",
      "en": "In Shaktism the three are manifestations of Mula Prakriti or Mahadevi. In Shaktism creator, preserver, and destroyer roles are assigned to Mahasaraswati, Mahalakshmi, and Mahakali.       "statement": {

      "id": "tridevi-c02",
      "source_id": "tridevi-s1",
      "quote": "The Tridevi form a trinity of supreme divinity in Hinduism, combining a triad of eminent goddesses either as a feminine version of the Trimurti, or as consorts of a masculine Trimurti, depending on the denomination.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian denominasi memandangnya sebagai versi perempuan Trimurti atau pasangan para dewa Trimurti.",
        "en": "Some denominations regard it as a feminine Trimurti or the Trimurti gods' consorts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tridevi-c03",
      "source_id": "tridevi-s1",
      "quote": "In Shaktism, these triune goddesses are the manifestations of Mula-Prakriti or Mahadevi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Shaktisme ketiganya merupakan manifestasi Mula Prakriti atau Mahadevi.",
        "en": "In Shaktism the three are manifestations of Mula Prakriti or Mahadevi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tridevi-c04",
      "source_id": "tridevi-s1",
      "quote": "In the traditional androcentric (masculine-centered) denominations of Hinduism, the feminine Tridevi goddesses are relegated as consorts and auxiliary deities to the masculine Trimurti gods.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Shaktisme peran pencipta, pemelihara, dan penghancur diberikan kepada Mahasaraswati, Mahalakshmi, dan Mahakali.",

    {
      "id": "tridevi-c05",
      "source_id": "tridevi-s1",
      "quote": "Saraswati is the goddess of learning, arts, and music, as well as the consort of Brahma, the creator.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saraswati adalah dewi pembelajaran, seni, dan musik serta pasangan Brahma.",
        "en": "Saraswati is goddess of learning, arts, and music and Brahma's consort."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tridevi-c06",
      "source_id": "tridevi-s2",
      "quote": "A trinity of goddesses is formed by Saraswati, the\r\nwhite one, Lakshmi, the red one, and Par´vati, the black\r\none. The three were originally one ... and divided herself, according to the\r\nVaraha Purana, into three forms representing the Past,\r\nPresent, and Future.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 827",

      "statement": {

        "en": "Mackenzie recounts a Varaha Purana version of one goddess dividing into Saraswati, Lakshmi, and Parvati."
      }

      "en": "Mahishasura is a bovine asura in Hinduism. The narrative depicts him using shape shifting to pursue evil intentions.",

    {

      "en": "He is called Rambha's son and Mahishi's brother. Durga kills him with a trident and gains the epithet Mahishasuramardini. Navaratri celebrates the battle and culminates in celebrating his defeat at Vijayadashami. In the narrative he obtains a boon that no man can kill him.",

      "quote": "is a bovine asura in Hinduism. He is depicted in Hindu literature as a deceitful demon who pursued his evil ways by shape-shifting.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

    {
      "id": "mahishasura-c02",
      "source_id": "mahishasura-s1",
      "quote": "Mahishasura (Sanskrit: महिषासुर, IAST: Mahiṣāsura), (Kannada: ಮಹಿಷಾಸುರ) is a bovine asura in Hinduism. He is depicted in Hindu literature as a deceitful demon who pursued his evil ways by shape-shifting. Mahishasura was the son of the asura Rambha and the brother of buffalo-demoness named Mahishi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Cerita menggambarkannya menggunakan perubahan wujud untuk menjalankan niat jahat.",
        "en": "The narrative depicts him using shape shifting to pursue evil intentions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahishasura-c03",
      "source_id": "mahishasura-s1",
      "quote": "Mahishasura was the son of the asura Rambha and the brother of buffalo-demoness named Mahishi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut putra Rambha dan saudara Mahishi.",
        "en": "He is called Rambha's son and Mahishi's brother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahishasura-c04",
      "source_id": "mahishasura-s1",
      "quote": "He was ultimately killed by the goddess Durga with her trishula (trident) after which she gained the epithet Mahishasuramardini (\"Slayer of Mahishasura\").",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Durga membunuhnya dengan trisula dan memperoleh epitet Mahishasuramardini.",
        "en": "Durga kills him with a trident and gains the epithet Mahishasuramardini."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahishasura-c05",
      "source_id": "mahishasura-s1",
      "quote": "The Navaratri (\"Nine Nights\") festival eulogises this battle between Mahishasura and Durga, culminating in Vijayadashami, a celebration of his ultimate defeat. This story of the \"triumph of good over evil\" carries profound symbolism in Hinduism, particularly Shaktism, and is both narrated as well as reenacted from the Devi Mahatmya at many South and Southeast Asian Hindu temples.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Navaratri merayakan pertempuran itu dan berakhir dengan perayaan kekalahannya pada Vijayadashami.",
        "en": "Navaratri celebrates the battle and culminates in celebrating his defeat at Vijayadashami."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahishasura-c06",
      "source_id": "mahishasura-s1",
      "quote": "Mahishasura had gained the boon that no man could kill him.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah, ia memperoleh anugerah bahwa tidak ada laki-laki yang dapat membunuhnya.",
        "en": "In the narrative he obtains a boon that no man can kill him."
      }
    }
  ],

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mohini adalah dewi pesona dan avatar perempuan Vishnu dalam Hindu. Dalam kisah pengadukan samudra ia membagi amrita kepada deva dan menghalangi asura memperolehnya.",
      "en": "Mohini is a Hindu goddess of enchantment and Vishnu's female avatar. In the ocean churning narrative she distributes amrita to devas and prevents asuras obtaining it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mohini-c01",
        "mohini-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisahnya mencakup pertemuan dengan Shiva dan penghancuran Bhasmasura. Ia terutama dipuja dalam bentuk Mhalsa di India Barat. Nama Mohini dikaitkan dengan akar moha yang berarti memperdaya atau memikat. Mackenzie menuturkan Vishnu mengambil wujud perempuan memikat sehingga asura menyerahkan amrita kepadanya.",
      "en": "Her narratives include an encounter with Shiva and the destruction of Bhasmasura. She is especially worshipped as Mhalsa in Western India. The name Mohini is linked to the root moha meaning delude or enchant. Mackenzie recounts Vishnu taking an enchanting female form so that the asuras give her amrita.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mohini-c03",
        "mohini-c04",
        "mohini-c05",
        "mohini-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mohini-c01",
      "source_id": "mohini-s1",
      "quote": "is the Hindu goddess of enchantment. She is the  only female avatar of the Hindu god Vishnu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mohini adalah dewi pesona dan avatar perempuan Vishnu dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "Mohini is a Hindu goddess of enchantment and Vishnu's female avatar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mohini-c02",
      "source_id": "mohini-s1",
      "quote": "a mesmerising beauty who distributes the amrita (the elixir of immortality) to the weakened devas (gods) and depriving it to the dominant asuras (demons),",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah pengadukan samudra ia membagi amrita kepada deva dan menghalangi asura memperolehnya.",
        "en": "In the ocean churning narrative she distributes amrita to devas and prevents asuras obtaining it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mohini-c03",
      "source_id": "mohini-s1",
      "quote": "Many different legends tell of her various exploits, including her famed encounter with the god Shiva. These tales relate, among other things, the birth of the god Shasta and the destruction of Bhasmasura, the ash-demon. Mohini's main modus operandi is to trick or beguile those she encounters.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya mencakup pertemuan dengan Shiva dan penghancuran Bhasmasura.",
        "en": "Her narratives include an encounter with Shiva and the destruction of Bhasmasura."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mohini-c04",
      "source_id": "mohini-s1",
      "quote": "She is worshipped throughout Indian culture, but mainly in Western India, where temples are devoted to her in the form of Mhalsa.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia terutama dipuja dalam bentuk Mhalsa di India Barat.",
        "en": "She is especially worshipped as Mhalsa in Western India."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mohini-c05",
      "source_id": "mohini-s1",
      "quote": "The name Mohini comes from the verb root moha, meaning \"to delude, enchant, perplex, or illusion,\" and literally means \"delusion personified.\" In the Baiga culture of Central India, the word mohini means \"erotic magic or spell.\" The name also has an implied connotation of \"the essence of female beauty and allurement.\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Mohini dikaitkan dengan akar moha yang berarti memperdaya atau memikat.",
        "en": "The name Mohini is linked to the root moha meaning delude or enchant."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mohini-c06",
      "source_id": "mohini-s2",
      "quote": "Meanwhile the demons desired to combat against the\r\ngods for the possession of the beautiful goddess Lakshmi\r\nand the amrita. But Vishnu assumed a bewitching female\r\nform, and so charmed the Asuras that they presented the\r\namrita to that fair woman.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 802",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menuturkan Vishnu mengambil wujud perempuan memikat sehingga asura menyerahkan amrita kepadanya.",
        "en": "Mackenzie recounts Vishnu taking an enchanting female form so that the asuras give her amrita."
      }
    }
  ],

  "gaps": [

      "id": "vajrapani-c01",
      "source_id": "vajrapani-s1",
      "quote": "is one of the earliest-appearing bodhisattvas in Mahayana Buddhism. He is the protector and guide of Gautama Buddha and rose to symbolize the Buddha's power.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vajrapani adalah bodhisatwa dalam Buddhisme Mahayana dan pelindung serta pembimbing Buddha Gautama.",

    },
    {
      "id": "vajrapani-c02",
      "source_id": "vajrapani-s1",
      "quote": "Vajrapāni is also called Chana Dorje and Chador",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Other names used are Chana Dorje and Chador."
      }

    {
      "id": "vajrapani-c03",
      "source_id": "vajrapani-s1",
      "quote": "Manjushri manifests all the Buddhas' wisdom, Avalokiteśvara manifests all the Buddhas' immense compassion, and Vajrapāni protects Buddha and manifests all the Buddhas' power",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melambangkan daya Buddha, bersama Manjushri yang melambangkan kebijaksanaan dan Avalokiteshvara yang melambangkan belas kasih.",
        "en": "He symbolizes the Buddha's power alongside Manjushri symbolizing wisdom and Avalokiteshvara symbolizing compassion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vajrapani-c04",
      "source_id": "vajrapani-s1",
      "quote": "Vajrapāni, \"holder of the thunderbolt\", is a compound word in Sanskrit in which 'Vajra' means \"Diamond or Thunderbolt\", and 'pāni' literally means \"in hand\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Sanskertanya dijelaskan sebagai pemegang vajra atau halilintar.",
        "en": "His Sanskrit name is explained as holder of the vajra or thunderbolt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vajrapani-c05",
      "source_id": "vajrapani-s1",
      "quote": "In human form Vajrapāni is depicted holding the vajra in his right hand.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wujud manusianya digambarkan memegang vajra di tangan kanan.",
        "en": "His human form is depicted holding a vajra in the right hand."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vajrapani-c06",
      "source_id": "vajrapani-s2",
      "quote": "In the tradition of Vajrayana Buddhism, Vajrapani is more typically shown in a wrathful form and known as Guhyapati - 'the Lord of Secrets.'",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "HAR menyatakan Vajrapani dalam tradisi Vajrayana sering digambarkan murka dan dikenal sebagai Guhyapati.",
        "en": "HAR states that Vajrapani in Vajrayana is often depicted wrathfully and known as Guhyapati."
      }
    }

      "id": "Indrani adalah ratu para deva dalam Hindu. Ia juga dikenal sebagai Shachi dan Paulomi.",
      "en": "Indrani is queen of the devas in Hinduism. She is also known as Shachi and Paulomi.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "indrani-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia adalah putri asura Puloman dan pasangan Indra. Dalam kisah Nahusha ia menyusun siasat menggulingkannya dan kembali kepada Indra. Indrani juga salah satu Sapta Matrika atau tujuh ibu ilahi. Mackenzie juga menyebut Indrani sebagai ratu dewa halilintar Indra.",

      "id": "indrani-c05",
      "source_id": "indrani-s1",
      "quote": "Indrani (or Aindri) is also one of the Sapta Matrikas—the seven divine mothers. She is an important goddess in Shaktism, a major sect of Hinduism.       "id": "Matrika adalah kelompok dewi ibu dalam Hindu. Kelompok tujuh disebut Saptamatrika dan kelompok delapan Ashtamatrika.",
      "en": "Matrikas are a group of mother goddesses in Hinduism. The seven member group is Saptamatrika and the eight member group Ashtamatrika.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "matrikas-c01",
        "matrikas-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para Matrika dikaitkan dengan para dewa sebagai energi atau Shakti mereka. Brahmani berasal dari Brahma dan Vaishnavi dari Vishnu. Dalam Shaktisme mereka membantu Durga melawan demon. Rujukan awal mengaitkan mereka dengan kehamilan, kelahiran, penyakit, dan perlindungan anak.",
      "en": "Matrikas are associated with gods as their energies or Shaktis. Brahmani emerges from Brahma and Vaishnavi from Vishnu. In Shaktism they assist Durga against demons. Early references associate them with conception, birth, diseases, and protecting children.",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

      "source_id": "matrikas-s1",
      "quote": "The Matrikas are often depicted in a group of seven, the Saptamatrika(s) (Seven Mothers). However, they are also depicted as a group of eight, the Ashtamatrika(s).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matrikas-c05",
      "source_id": "matrikas-s1",
      "quote": "In Shaktism, they are described as assisting the Durga in her fight with demons and demonesses and killing all of them.",

      "id": "matrikas-c06",
      "source_id": "matrikas-s1",
      "quote": "In most early references, the Matrikas are associated with the conception, birth, diseases, protection of children.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Prajapati adalah dewa agama Weda kuno yang diidentifikasi dengan Brahma dalam Hindu.",
    "en": "Prajapati is an ancient Vedic deity identified with Brahma in Hinduism.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "prajapati-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Prajapati adalah dewa agama Weda kuno yang diidentifikasi dengan Brahma dalam Hindu. Dalam tradisi Purana gelar ini diberikan kepada berbagai dewa yang berperan dalam penciptaan.",
      "en": "Prajapati is an ancient Vedic deity identified with Brahma in Hinduism. In Puranic tradition the title applies to various deities involved in creation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "prajapati-c01",
        "prajapati-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sastra klasik dan abad pertengahan juga memakai Prajapati dalam arti metafisis Brahman. Nama Prajapati menggabungkan praja dan pati dalam makna penguasa makhluk. Maknanya berkembang menjadi sebutan bagi makhluk ilahi, setengah dewa, atau resi pencipta sesuatu. Mackenzie menyebut Prajapati dan Narayana sebagai nama inkarnasi ilahi Brahma.",
      "en": "Classical and medieval literature also uses Prajapati in the metaphysical sense of Brahman. The name Prajapati combines praja and pati meaning lord of creatures. Its meaning develops into a designation for divine beings, demigods, or sages creating something. Mackenzie gives Prajapati and Narayana as names of Brahma's divine incarnation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "prajapati-c03",
        "prajapati-c04",
        "prajapati-c05",
        "prajapati-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],
  "places": [],

  "learning_questions": [],
  "images": [],
  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "prajapati-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prajapati",
      "title": "Prajapati",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,

      "type": "wikipedia",

    },
    {
      "id": "prajapati-s2",
      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/47228/pg47228-images.html",
      "title": "The Project Gutenberg eBook of Indian Myth and Legend, by Donald A. Mackenzie.",
      "author": "Donald A. Mackenzie",
      "publisher": "The Gresham Publishing Company; Project Gutenberg",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "folklore-collection",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "prajapati-c01",
      "source_id": "prajapati-s1",
      "quote": "Prajapati (Sanskrit: प्रजापति, lit. 'Lord of the people', IAST: Prajāpati) is an important deity in ancient Vedic religion, who is identified with Brahma, the creator god in Hinduism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Prajapati adalah dewa agama Weda kuno yang diidentifikasi dengan Brahma dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "Prajapati is an ancient Vedic deity identified with Brahma in Hinduism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "prajapati-c02",
      "source_id": "prajapati-s1",
      "quote": "In Puranic tradition, the term is a name applied to many different deities of Hinduism that played roles in creation.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Purana gelar ini diberikan kepada berbagai dewa yang berperan dalam penciptaan.",
        "en": "In Puranic tradition the title applies to various deities involved in creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "prajapati-c03",
      "source_id": "prajapati-s1",
      "quote": "In classical and medieval era literature, Prajapati is the metaphysical concept called Brahman as Prajapati-Brahman, and Brahman is the primordial matter that made Prajapati.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sastra klasik dan abad pertengahan juga memakai Prajapati dalam arti metafisis Brahman.",
        "en": "Classical and medieval literature also uses Prajapati in the metaphysical sense of Brahman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "prajapati-c04",
      "source_id": "prajapati-s1",
      "quote": "Prajapati (Sanskrit: प्रजापति) is a compound of \"praja\" (creation, procreative powers) and \"pati\" (lord, master). The term means \"lord of creatures\", or \"lord of all born beings\". In the later Vedic texts, Prajapati is a distinct Vedic deity, but whose significance diminishes. Later, the term is synonymous with other gods, particularly Brahma.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Prajapati menggabungkan praja dan pati dalam makna penguasa makhluk.",
        "en": "The name Prajapati combines praja and pati meaning lord of creatures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "prajapati-c05",
      "source_id": "prajapati-s1",
      "quote": "Still later, the term evolved to mean any divine or semi-divine being or human sage who created something new.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maknanya berkembang menjadi sebutan bagi makhluk ilahi, setengah dewa, atau resi pencipta sesuatu.",
        "en": "Its meaning develops into a designation for divine beings, demigods, or sages creating something."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "prajapati-c06",
      "source_id": "prajapati-s2",
      "quote": "Various creation myths were framed by teachers to\r\nsatisfy the desire for knowledge regarding the beginning\r\nof things. The divine incarnation of Brahmă is known\r\nas Brahma (masculine) Prajapati, and Nãrãyana.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 613",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menyebut Prajapati dan Narayana sebagai nama inkarnasi ilahi Brahma.",
        "en": "Mackenzie gives Prajapati and Narayana as names of Brahma's divine incarnation."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ushas adalah dewi fajar Weda dalam Hindu. Himne menggambarkannya membawa cahaya dan mengusir kegelapan serta demon.",
      "en": "Ushas is a Vedic dawn goddess in Hinduism. Hymns depict her bringing light and driving away darkness and demons.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ushas-c01",
        "ushas-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai perempuan muda berhiaskan perhiasan dan menaiki kereta emas. Ratri atau Nisha, dewi malam, disebut sebagai saudarinya. Kata ushás diturunkan dari usha yang berarti fajar. Mackenzie menyebut Ushas sebagai putri Dyaus dan personifikasi fajar.",
      "en": "She is depicted as an adorned young woman riding a golden chariot. Ratri or Nisha, the night deity, is called her sister. The word ushas derives from usha meaning dawn. Mackenzie calls Ushas Dyaus's daughter and personification of dawn.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ushas-c03",
        "ushas-c04",
        "ushas-c05",
        "ushas-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ushas-c01",
      "source_id": "ushas-s1",
      "quote": "is a Vedic goddess of dawn in Hinduism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ushas adalah dewi fajar Weda dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "Ushas is a Vedic dawn goddess in Hinduism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ushas-c02",
      "source_id": "ushas-s1",
      "quote": "consistently identified with dawn, revealing herself with the daily coming of light to the world, driving away oppressive darkness, chasing away evil demons,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Himne menggambarkannya membawa cahaya dan mengusir kegelapan serta demon.",
        "en": "Hymns depict her bringing light and driving away darkness and demons."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ushas-c03",
      "source_id": "ushas-s1",
      "quote": "She is portrayed as a beautifully adorned young woman riding in a golden chariot or a hundred chariots,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai perempuan muda berhiaskan perhiasan dan menaiki kereta emas.",
        "en": "She is depicted as an adorned young woman riding a golden chariot."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ushas-c04",
      "source_id": "ushas-s1",
      "quote": "Her sister is \"Nisha\" or Ratri, the deity of night.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ratri atau Nisha, dewi malam, disebut sebagai saudarinya.",
        "en": "Ratri or Nisha, the night deity, is called her sister."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ushas-c05",
      "source_id": "ushas-s1",
      "quote": "Vedic uṣás is derived from the word uṣá which means \"dawn\". This word comes from Proto-Indo-Iranian *Hušā́s (ušā in Avestan), which in turn is from Proto-Indo-European *h₂éwsōs (\"dawn\"), and is related to ēṓs in Greek and aušrà in Lithuanian. It is also the basis for the word \"east\" in Indo-European traditions, state Mallory and Adams.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ushás diturunkan dari usha yang berarti fajar.",
        "en": "The word ushas derives from usha meaning dawn."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ushas-c06",
      "source_id": "ushas-s2",
      "quote": "The effulgence and silence of dawn inspired some of\r\nthe most beautiful Vedic hymns. Dawn is Ushas, the\r\ndaughter of Dyaus; she is the Indian Aurora:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 265",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menyebut Ushas sebagai putri Dyaus dan personifikasi fajar.",
        "en": "Mackenzie calls Ushas Dyaus's daughter and personification of dawn."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Jatayu adalah setengah dewa berwujud burung nasar dalam Ramayana. Ia merupakan putra Aruna dan Shyeni, saudara Sampati, dan keponakan Garuda.",
      "en": "Jatayu is a demigod in vulture form in the Ramayana. He is Aruna and Shyeni's son, Sampati's brother, and Garuda's nephew.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Jatayu merupakan sahabat lama Dasharatha, ayah Rama. Dalam kisah masa mudanya, Sampati melindunginya dari panas Surya dan terbakar sayapnya. Jatayu berusaha menyelamatkan Sita dari penculikan Ravana tetapi dikalahkan. Mackenzie menuturkan Jatayu yang sekarat memberi tahu Rama apa yang terjadi pada Sita.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jatayu-c01",
      "source_id": "jatayu-s1",
      "quote": "is a demigod in the Hindu epic Rāmayana, who has the form of a vulture.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jatayu adalah setengah dewa berwujud burung nasar dalam Ramayana.",
        "en": "Jatayu is a demigod in vulture form in the Ramayana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatayu-c02",
      "source_id": "jatayu-s1",
      "quote": "Jatayu (Sanskrit: जटायु, IAST: Jaṭāyu) is a demigod in the Hindu epic Rāmayana, who has the form of a vulture. He was the younger son of Aruṇa and his wife Shyeni, the brother of Sampati, and the nephew of Garuda. He was also an old friend of King Dasharatha, Rāma's father.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia merupakan putra Aruna dan Shyeni, saudara Sampati, dan keponakan Garuda.",
        "en": "He is Aruna and Shyeni's son, Sampati's brother, and Garuda's nephew."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatayu-c03",
      "source_id": "jatayu-s1",
      "quote": "Jatayu (Sanskrit: जटायु, IAST: Jaṭāyu) is a demigod in the Hindu epic Rāmayana, who has the form of a vulture. He was the younger son of Aruṇa and his wife Shyeni, the brother of Sampati, and the nephew of Garuda. He was also an old friend of King Dasharatha, Rāma's father.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jatayu merupakan sahabat lama Dasharatha, ayah Rama.",
        "en": "Jatayu is an old friend of Dasharatha, Rama's father."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatayu-c04",
      "source_id": "jatayu-s1",
      "quote": "In a desperate bid to rescue his brother, Sampati flew ahead of him, spreading his wings wide open to shield him. As a consequence, it was Sampati who had his wings burnt,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah masa mudanya, Sampati melindunginya dari panas Surya dan terbakar sayapnya.",
        "en": "In the youth narrative Sampati shields him from Surya's heat and has his own wings burned."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatayu-c05",
      "source_id": "jatayu-s1",
      "quote": "According to the epic, the rakshasa Ravana was abducting the avatar of Lakshmi, Sita, when Jatayu tried to rescue her. Jatayu fought valiantly with Ravana, but as Jatayu was very old, Ravana soon defeated him,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jatayu berusaha menyelamatkan Sita dari penculikan Ravana tetapi dikalahkan.",
        "en": "Jatayu tries to save Sita from Ravana's abduction but is defeated."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatayu-c06",
      "source_id": "jatayu-s2",
      "quote": "On the morrow the brethren went forth again in\r\nquest of the lost one. They came to the place where\r\nJatayus lay dying, and that lordly bird spake to Rama\r\nand related all that had befallen Sita and himself.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2297",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menuturkan Jatayu yang sekarat memberi tahu Rama apa yang terjadi pada Sita.",
        "en": "Mackenzie recounts dying Jatayu telling Rama what happened to Sita."
      }
    }
  ],

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Vasuki adalah raja naga dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "Vasuki is a king of nagas in Hinduism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vasuki-c02",
      "source_id": "vasuki-s1",
      "quote": "Vasuki (Sanskrit: वासुकि, romanized: Vāsuki) is the king of the nagas in Hinduism. He is described as having a gem called Nāgamani (serpent's ornament) on his head. Shesha, another king of the nagas and the bed on which Vishnu rests, is his elder brother, and Manasa, another naga, is his sister.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan memiliki permata Nagamani di kepalanya.",
        "en": "He is described as having a Nagamani gem on his head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vasuki-c03",
      "source_id": "vasuki-s1",
      "quote": "Shesha, another king of the nagas and the bed on which Vishnu rests, is his elder brother, and Manasa, another naga, is his sister.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shesha disebut kakaknya dan Manasa saudarinya.",
        "en": "Shesha is called his elder brother and Manasa his sister."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vasuki-c04",
      "source_id": "vasuki-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu iconography, he is generally depicted coiling around the neck of Shiva, who is believed to have blessed and worn him as an ornament.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam ikonografi Hindu ia melilit leher Shiva.",
        "en": "In Hindu iconography he coils around Shiva's neck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vasuki-c05",
      "source_id": "vasuki-s1",
      "quote": "He is accorded a significant role in the legend of Samudra Manthana. He is described to have allowed both the devas and the asuras to bind him to Mount Mandara, so that they could use him as their churning rope to extract the amrita from the Ocean of Milk.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi tali pengaduk samudra susu yang dipakai para deva dan asura untuk memperoleh amrita.",
        "en": "He becomes the milk ocean churning rope used by devas and asuras to obtain amrita."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vasuki-c06",
      "source_id": "vasuki-s2",
      "quote": "Bhima found that he had sunk down to the city of\r\nserpents, which is in the underworld. Vasuki, king of\r\nthe Nagas, having heard of his prowess, hastened towards\r\nthe young warrior, whom he desired greatly to\r\nbehold.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 985",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam retelling Mackenzie, Vasuki menyambut Bhima di kota ular bawah tanah.",
        "en": "In Mackenzie's retelling Vasuki greets Bhima in the underworld serpent city."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Chamunda adalah wujud ganas Chandi atau Mahadevi dan anggota tujuh Matrika. Ia juga salah satu Yogini yang mengiringi Parvati.",
      "en": "Chamunda is a fierce form of Chandi or Mahadevi and a member of the seven Matrikas. She is also one of the Yoginis attending Parvati.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chamunda-c01",
        "chamunda-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya menggabungkan nama Chanda dan Munda, dua demon yang dibunuhnya. Ia sering digambarkan tinggal di tempat pembakaran jenazah atau dekat pohon ara suci. Penggambaran belakangan memperlihatkannya sebagai perempuan tua menyerupai kerangka dengan mata cekung. Ia digambarkan berdiri atau duduk di atas jasad atau demon yang dikalahkan.",
      "en": "Her name combines Chanda and Munda, two demons she kills. She is often depicted living in cremation grounds or near sacred fig trees. Later depictions show her as a skeletal old woman with sunken eyes. She is depicted standing or sitting on a corpse or a vanquished demon.",

        "chamunda-c03",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "chamunda-c01",
      "source_id": "chamunda-s1",
      "quote": "Chamunda (Sanskrit: चामुण्डा, IAST: Cāmuṇḍā), also known as Chamundeshwari, Chamundi or is a fearsome form of Chandi, the Hindu mother goddess, Mahadevi and is one of the seven Matrikas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chamunda adalah wujud ganas Chandi atau Mahadevi dan anggota tujuh Matrika.",
        "en": "Chamunda is a fierce form of Chandi or Mahadevi and a member of the seven Matrikas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chamunda-c02",
      "source_id": "chamunda-s1",
      "quote": "She is also one of the chief Yoginis, a group of sixty-four or eighty-one Tantric goddesses, who are attendants of the warrior goddess Parvati. The name is a combination of Chanda and Munda, two demons whom Chamunda killed. She is closely associated with Mahakali or Durga.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga salah satu Yogini yang mengiringi Parvati.",
        "en": "She is also one of the Yoginis attending Parvati."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chamunda-c03",
      "source_id": "chamunda-s1",
      "quote": "She is also one of the chief Yoginis, a group of sixty-four or eighty-one Tantric goddesses, who are attendants of the warrior goddess Parvati. The name is a combination of Chanda and Munda, two demons whom Chamunda killed. She is closely associated with Mahakali or Durga.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya menggabungkan nama Chanda dan Munda, dua demon yang dibunuhnya.",
        "en": "Her name combines Chanda and Munda, two demons she kills."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chamunda-c04",
      "source_id": "chamunda-s1",
      "quote": "The goddess is often portrayed as residing in cremation grounds or near holy fig trees. The goddess is worshipped by ritual animal sacrifices along with offerings of wine. The practice of animal sacrifices has become less common with Vaishnavite influences.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia sering digambarkan tinggal di tempat pembakaran jenazah atau dekat pohon ara suci.",
        "en": "She is often depicted living in cremation grounds or near sacred fig trees."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chamunda-c05",
      "source_id": "chamunda-s1",
      "quote": "In  early representations of Chamunda, she is given a ferocious visage, but with a feminine figure and elegant features. However, in later representations she is generally depicted as a skeletal old women wearing an elephant or tiger hide. She is shown to have sunken eyes, dishevelled hair.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran belakangan memperlihatkannya sebagai perempuan tua menyerupai kerangka dengan mata cekung.",
        "en": "Later depictions show her as a skeletal old woman with sunken eyes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chamunda-c06",
      "source_id": "chamunda-s1",
      "quote": "She is standing or sitting upon a 'shava' or corpse or sitting on a 'preta' or vanquished demon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan berdiri atau duduk di atas jasad atau demon yang dikalahkan.",
        "en": "She is depicted standing or sitting on a corpse or a vanquished demon."

      "en": "Kinnara is a Hindu and Buddhist mythological creature depicted as part human and part bird. The creature is closely associated with music and love.",

Schafer membedakan kinnara dari kalavinka meski keduanya sering tertukar dalam seni Asia Timur. Dalam kisah Tapati yang diceritakan Mackenzie, suara sang tokoh dipuji seindah nyanyian Kinnara.",

      "source_id": "kinnara-s1",
      "quote": "A kinnara (Sanskrit: किन्नर Kiṃnara) is a creature from Hindu and Buddhist mythology.  They are described as part human and part bird, and have a strong association with music and love.     {

      "source_id": "kinnara-s1",

    {
      "id": "kinnara-c05",
      "source_id": "kinnara-s1",
      "quote": "Edward H. Schafer notes that in East Asian religious art, the kinnara is often confused with the Kalaviṅka, which is also a half-human, half-bird hybrid creature, but that the two are actually distinct and unrelated.",

    "claim_ids": [

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Preta merupakan makhluk gaib yang dalam tradisi agama Asia mengalami penderitaan serta lapar dan haus ekstrem. Sebutan hantu lapar terutama sesuai adaptasi Asia Timur; Petavatthu awal memuat bentuk yang lebih beragam.",
      "en": "Preta is a supernatural being suffering extreme hunger and thirst in Asian religious traditions. The term hungry ghost mainly fits East Asian adaptations; the early Petavatthu has more varied forms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "preta-c01",
        "preta-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam keyakinan yang dibahas, karma kehidupan lalu mengakibatkan rasa lapar yang tak terpuaskan. Tradisi preta berasal dari agama India dan menyebar ke Asia Timur melalui Buddhisme. Preta dalam bahasa Sanskerta berarti orang yang telah pergi atau meninggal. Dalam Tirokudda Kanda, hantu lapar hidup dari pemberian orang yang masih hidup.",
      "en": "In the discussed belief past life karma causes insatiable hunger. Preta traditions originate in Indian religions and spread to East Asia through Buddhism. Preta in Sanskrit means a departed or deceased person. In the Tirokudda Kanda, hungry shades live on gifts from the living.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "preta-c03",
        "preta-c04",

    {

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "preta-c01",
      "source_id": "preta-s1",
      "quote": "is the Sanskrit name for a type of supernatural being described in Hinduism, Buddhism, Taoism, and Chinese folk religion as undergoing suffering greater than that of humans, particularly an extreme level of hunger and thirst.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Preta merupakan makhluk gaib yang dalam tradisi agama Asia mengalami penderitaan serta lapar dan haus ekstrem.",
        "en": "Preta is a supernatural being suffering extreme hunger and thirst in Asian religious traditions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "preta-c02",
      "source_id": "preta-s1",
      "quote": "Preta is often translated into English as \"hungry ghost\" from the Chinese and East Asian adaptations. In early sources such as the Petavatthu, they are much more varied. The descriptions below apply mainly in this narrower context.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutan hantu lapar terutama sesuai adaptasi Asia Timur; Petavatthu awal memuat bentuk yang lebih beragam.",
        "en": "The term hungry ghost mainly fits East Asian adaptations; the early Petavatthu has more varied forms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "preta-c03",
      "source_id": "preta-s1",
      "quote": "As a result of their karma, they are afflicted with an insatiable hunger for a particular substance or object.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam keyakinan yang dibahas, karma kehidupan lalu mengakibatkan rasa lapar yang tak terpuaskan.",
        "en": "In the discussed belief past life karma causes insatiable hunger."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "preta-c04",
      "source_id": "preta-s1",
      "quote": "Preta have their origins in Indian religions and have been adopted into East Asian religions via the spread of Buddhism. Through the belief and influence of Hinduism and Buddhism in much of Asia, preta figure appear prominently in the cultures of India, Bangladesh, Sri Lanka, China, Japan, Korea, Vietnam, Mongolia, Tibet, Thailand, Cambodia, Laos and Myanmar.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi preta berasal dari agama India dan menyebar ke Asia Timur melalui Buddhisme.",
        "en": "Preta traditions originate in Indian religions and spread to East Asia through Buddhism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "preta-c05",
      "source_id": "preta-s1",
      "quote": "The Sanskrit term प्रेत preta means \"departed, deceased, a dead person\", from pra-ita, literally \"gone forth, departed\". In Classical Sanskrit, the term refers to the spirit of any dead person, but especially before the obsequial rites are performed, but also more narrowly to a ghost or evil being.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Preta dalam bahasa Sanskerta berarti orang yang telah pergi atau meninggal.",
        "en": "Preta in Sanskrit means a departed or deceased person."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "preta-c06",
      "source_id": "preta-s2",
      "quote": "They live on what is given here,\n\thungry shades",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 72",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Tirokudda Kanda, hantu lapar hidup dari pemberian orang yang masih hidup.",
        "en": "In the Tirokudda Kanda, hungry shades live on gifts from the living."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Vritra adalah danava yang mempersonifikasikan kekeringan dan menjadi lawan Indra. Ia tampil sebagai ular menyerupai manusia yang menghalangi aliran sungai Rigveda.",
      "en": "Vritra is a danava personifying drought and opposing Indra. He appears as a human like serpent blocking Rigvedic rivers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vritra-c01",
        "vritra-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dibunuh Indra dengan vajra yang baru dibuat. Namanya dijelaskan berarti penutup atau penghalang, berkaitan dengan penahanan air. Kisah pertempuran menyebut Vritra mematahkan rahang Indra sebelum dikalahkan. Retelling Mackenzie menggambarkan Vritra menahan ternak awan dalam benteng gunungnya.",
      "en": "Indra kills him with a newly forged vajra. His name is explained as cover or obstacle, linked to holding back waters.       "claim_ids": [
        "vritra-c03",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vritra-c01",
      "source_id": "vritra-s1",
      "quote": "He serves as the personification of drought, and is an adversary of the king of the devas, Indra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vritra adalah danava yang mempersonifikasikan kekeringan dan menjadi lawan Indra.",
        "en": "Vritra is a danava personifying drought and opposing Indra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vritra-c02",
      "source_id": "vritra-s1",
      "quote": "He appears as a human-like serpent blocking the course of the Rigvedic rivers, and is slain by Indra with his newly forged vajra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tampil sebagai ular menyerupai manusia yang menghalangi aliran sungai Rigveda.",
        "en": "He appears as a human like serpent blocking Rigvedic rivers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vritra-c03",
      "source_id": "vritra-s1",
      "quote": "He appears as a human-like serpent blocking the course of the Rigvedic rivers, and is slain by Indra with his newly forged vajra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dibunuh Indra dengan vajra yang baru dibuat.",
        "en": "Indra kills him with a newly forged vajra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vritra-c04",
      "source_id": "vritra-s1",
      "quote": "Vritra literally means \"cover, obstacle\", in reference of him holding back the waters. It stems from Proto-Indo-Iranian *wr̥trás, from the Proto-Indo-European root *wer- \"to cover, to obstruct\". The Indo-Iranian word is also found in Avestan as vərəθraγna (Vedic vṛtraghná), literally \"(one who) slays obstacles\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dijelaskan berarti penutup atau penghalang, berkaitan dengan penahanan air.",
        "en": "His name is explained as cover or obstacle, linked to holding back waters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vritra-c05",
      "source_id": "vritra-s1",
      "quote": "Vritra broke Indra's two jaws during the battle, but was then thrown down by Indra and, in falling, crushed the fortresses that had already been shattered. For this feat, Indra became known as \"Vṛtrahan\" (lit. \"Slayer of Vritra\" and also as \"slayer of the first-born of dragons\").",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah pertempuran menyebut Vritra mematahkan rahang Indra sebelum dikalahkan.",
        "en": "The battle narrative says Vritra breaks Indra's jaws before being defeated."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vritra-c06",
      "source_id": "vritra-s2",
      "quote": "According to Vedic myth, Indra achieved his first\r\ngreat victory immediately after birth. Vritra, “the encompasser”,\r\nthe Demon of Drought, was holding captive\r\nin his mountain fortress the cloud-cattle which he had\r\nharried in the approved manner of the Aryan raiders.[43]\r\nMankind entreated the aid of the gods, “the shining\r\nones, the world guardians”:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 132",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Retelling Mackenzie menggambarkan Vritra menahan ternak awan dalam benteng gunungnya.",
        "en": "Mackenzie's retelling depicts Vritra holding cloud cattle captive in his mountain fortress."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "Yamuna is the goddess personifying the Yamuna river in Hinduism. Early texts call her Yami and later literature Kalindi.",

        "yamuna-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia disebut putri Surya dan Sanjna serta saudari kembar Yama. Ia merupakan salah satu delapan pasangan utama Krishna. Agni Purana menggambarkannya berkulit gelap, bertunggangan kura-kura, dan membawa wadah air. Mackenzie mengutip himne yang menuturkan Yami mengajak saudaranya Yama menikah.",
      "en": "She is called Surya and Sanjna's daughter and Yama's twin sister. She is one of Krishna's eight principal consorts. The Agni Purana depicts her as dark, mounted on a tortoise, and holding a water pot. Mackenzie quotes a hymn recounting Yami asking her brother Yama to marry.",

      "source_id": "yamuna-s1",

    },
    {
      "id": "yamuna-c03",
      "source_id": "yamuna-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu scriptures, she is the daughter of Surya, the sun god, and Sanjna, the cloud goddess. She is also the twin sister of Yama, god of death.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      "source_id": "yamuna-s1",

      "id": "yamuna-c05",
      "source_id": "yamuna-s1",
      "quote": "Yamuna's iconographic depiction is seen on temple doorjambs, paired with that of Ganga (the goddess of the Ganges), since the Gupta era.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "Kamadhenu atau Surabhi adalah dewi sapi dan ibu semua sapi dalam Hindu. Ia merupakan sapi ajaib yang menyediakan apa pun yang diinginkan pemiliknya.",
      "en": "Kamadhenu or Surabhi is a bovine goddess and mother of all cows in Hinduism. She is a miraculous cow providing whatever her owner desires.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kamadhenu-c01",
        "kamadhenu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Salah satu ikonografinya berupa sapi putih berkepala perempuan, bersayap burung, dan berekor merak. Kisah asalnya berbeda: muncul dari pengadukan samudra atau menjadi putri Daksha dan istri Kashyapa. Ia menghasilkan susu untuk persembahan sang resi dan dapat menghasilkan prajurit untuk melindunginya. Sebutan Kamadhenu berarti sapi yang menyediakan semua yang diinginkan; Nandini kadang mendapat sebutan itu atau disebut putrinya.",
      "en": "One iconographic form is a white cow with a woman's head, bird wings, and peafowl tail. Kamadhenu means a cow providing all that is desired; Nandini sometimes bears the epithet or is called her daughter.",

      "source_id": "kamadhenu-s1",
      "quote": "she is generally depicted as a white cow with a female head and breasts, the wings of a bird, and the tail of a peafowl",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu ikonografinya berupa sapi putih berkepala perempuan, bersayap burung, dan berekor merak.",
        "en": "One iconographic form is a white cow with a woman's head, bird wings, and peafowl tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamadhenu-c04",
      "source_id": "kamadhenu-s1",
      "quote": "Hindu scriptures provide diverse accounts of the birth of Kamadhenu. While some narrate that she emerged from the churning of the cosmic ocean, others describe her as the daughter of the creator god Daksha, and as the wife of the sage Kashyapa.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah asalnya berbeda: muncul dari pengadukan samudra atau menjadi putri Daksha dan istri Kashyapa.",
        "en": "Her origin accounts differ: emerging from ocean churning or being Daksha's daughter and Kashyapa's wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamadhenu-c05",
      "source_id": "kamadhenu-s1",
      "quote": "Kamadhenu plays the important role of providing milk and milk products to be used in her sage-master's oblations; she is also capable of producing fierce warriors to protect him.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menghasilkan susu untuk persembahan sang resi dan dapat menghasilkan prajurit untuk melindunginya.",
        "en": "She produces milk for her sage master's offerings and can produce warriors to protect him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamadhenu-c06",
      "source_id": "kamadhenu-s1",
      "quote": "In the Mahabharata and Devi Bhagavata Purana, in the context of the birth of Bhishma, the cow Nandini is given the epithet Kamadhenu. In other instances, Nandini is described as the cow-daughter of Surabhi-Kamadhenu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutan Kamadhenu berarti sapi yang menyediakan semua yang diinginkan; Nandini kadang mendapat sebutan itu atau disebut putrinya.",
        "en": "Kamadhenu means a cow providing all that is desired; Nandini sometimes bears the epithet or is called her daughter."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

    "claim_ids": [
      "vahana-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vahana adalah golongan makhluk, biasanya hewan atau makhluk mitos, yang menjadi kendaraan dewa Hindu. Dewa digambarkan menaikinya atau vahana berada di samping dewa sebagai atribut.",
      "en": "Vahana denotes beings, usually animals or mythical creatures, serving as Hindu deities' vehicles. Deities are depicted riding them or vahanas stand beside deities as attributes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vahana-c01",
        "vahana-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Akar Sanskerta vah berarti membawa atau mengangkut. Nandi sebagai kendaraan Shiva melambangkan kekuatan; hamsa Saraswati melambangkan kebijaksanaan. Kendaraan ilahi juga dapat melambangkan kekuatan negatif yang ditaklukkan dewanya. Dalam retelling Mackenzie, gajah putih Airavata menjadi tunggangan Indra.",
      "en": "The Sanskrit root vah means to carry or transport. Nandi as Shiva's vehicle symbolizes strength; Saraswati's hamsa symbolizes wisdom. Divine vehicles can also symbolize negative forces overcome by their deities. In Mackenzie's retelling the white elephant Airavata is Indra's mount.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vahana-c03",
        "vahana-c04",
        "vahana-c05",
        "vahana-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vahana-c01",
      "source_id": "vahana-s1",
      "quote": "denotes the being, typically an animal or mythical entity, a particular Hindu deity is said to use as a vehicle.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vahana adalah golongan makhluk, biasanya hewan atau makhluk mitos, yang menjadi kendaraan dewa Hindu.",
        "en": "Vahana denotes beings, usually animals or mythical creatures, serving as Hindu deities' vehicles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vahana-c02",
      "source_id": "vahana-s1",
      "quote": "Deities are often depicted riding (or simply mounted upon) the vāhana. Other times, the vāhana is depicted at the deity's side or symbolically represented as a divine attribute.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewa digambarkan menaikinya atau vahana berada di samping dewa sebagai atribut.",
        "en": "Deities are depicted riding them or vahanas stand beside deities as attributes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vahana-c03",
      "source_id": "vahana-s1",
      "quote": "Vah in Sanskrit means to carry or transport. The word also means 'vehicle' in Sanskrit and other Indian languages.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akar Sanskerta vah berarti membawa atau mengangkut.",
        "en": "The Sanskrit root vah means to carry or transport."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vahana-c04",
      "source_id": "vahana-s1",
      "quote": "Nandi the bull and vehicle of Shiva, represents strength and virility. ... The hamsa, the vehicle of Saraswati, represents wisdom, grace, and beauty.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nandi sebagai kendaraan Shiva melambangkan kekuatan; hamsa Saraswati melambangkan kebijaksanaan.",
        "en": "Nandi as Shiva's vehicle symbolizes strength; Saraswati's hamsa symbolizes wisdom."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vahana-c05",
      "source_id": "vahana-s1",
      "quote": "However, a divine mount also symbolizes the negative or destructive forces over which the deity triumphs. Mounted on Parvani, Kartikeya reins in the peacock's vanity. Seated upon Dinka, the rat (Mushika), Ganesha subdues erratic thoughts, which multiply like rodents in the dark. Shani, the protector of property, rides a vulture, raven, or crow, whose thieving tendencies he represents.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kendaraan ilahi juga dapat melambangkan kekuatan negatif yang ditaklukkan dewanya.",
        "en": "Divine vehicles can also symbolize negative forces overcome by their deities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vahana-c06",
      "source_id": "vahana-s2",
      "quote": "Indra was attended by a dog, as befitted a deity of[Pg 18]\r\nprimitive huntsmen. After the early Aryan period, he\r\nshowed less favour for his bays and chariot, and seated\r\nhimself upon a great white elephant, “the handsome and\r\never victorious”, named Airavata; it “was furnished with\r\nfour tusks” and “resembled the mountain of Kailasa with\r\nits summits”.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 178",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam retelling Mackenzie, gajah putih Airavata menjadi tunggangan Indra.",
        "en": "In Mackenzie's retelling the white elephant Airavata is Indra's mount."
      }
    }
  ],

    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Indrajit atau Meghanada adalah putra sulung Ravana dan putra mahkota Lanka dalam Ramayana. Ia dapat bertempur dari langit sambil tersembunyi di balik awan.",
      "en": "Indrajit or Meghanada is Ravana's eldest son and Lanka's crown prince in the Ramayana. He can fight from the sky while hidden behind clouds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "indrajit-c01",
        "indrajit-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Indrajit berarti penakluk Indra, berkaitan dengan kemenangannya atas raja para deva. Ibunya adalah Mandodari, istri Ravana. Sulochana disebut sebagai istrinya dalam versi belakangan, bukan dalam epos asli. Mackenzie mengisahkan Indrajit membelenggu Hanuman dengan anak panah sihir berwujud ular.",
      "en": "Indrajit means conqueror of Indra, linked to his victory over the deva king. His mother is Mandodari, Ravana's wife. Sulochana is called his wife in later versions rather than the original epic. Mackenzie recounts Indrajit binding Hanuman with a magical serpent arrow.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "indrajit-c03",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "indrajit-c01",
      "source_id": "indrajit-s1",
      "quote": "was the eldest son of Ravana and the crown prince of Lanka,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Indrajit atau Meghanada adalah putra sulung Ravana dan putra mahkota Lanka dalam Ramayana.",
        "en": "Indrajit or Meghanada is Ravana's eldest son and Lanka's crown prince in the Ramayana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "indrajit-c02",
      "source_id": "indrajit-s1",
      "quote": "Indrajita had the special ability to fight from the sky, hidden behind the clouds.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dapat bertempur dari langit sambil tersembunyi di balik awan.",
        "en": "He can fight from the sky while hidden behind clouds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "indrajit-c03",
      "source_id": "indrajit-s1",
      "quote": "Indrajita had the special ability to fight from the sky, hidden behind the clouds. That is why both Rama and Lakshmana were defeated during the battle and were tied up by the snake. In Sanskrit, the literal translation of the name \"Indrajita\" (इन्द्रजित) is mentioned as the \"Conqueror of Indra\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Indrajit berarti penakluk Indra, berkaitan dengan kemenangannya atas raja para deva.",
        "en": "Indrajit means conqueror of Indra, linked to his victory over the deva king."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "indrajit-c04",
      "source_id": "indrajit-s1",
      "quote": "Indrajita was the eldest son of Ravana and his wife Mandodari.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ibunya adalah Mandodari, istri Ravana.",
        "en": "His mother is Mandodari, Ravana's wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "indrajit-c05",
      "source_id": "indrajit-s1",
      "quote": "His wife is not mentioned in the original epic; however in later versions of the epic, Sulochana",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sulochana disebut sebagai istrinya dalam versi belakangan, bukan dalam epos asli.",
        "en": "Sulochana is called his wife in later versions rather than the original epic."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "indrajit-c06",
      "source_id": "indrajit-s2",
      "quote": "Hanuman desired, ere he left the city of Ravana, to\r\nshow his enmity against the demons. Assuming his\r\ngigantic form, he uprooted trees and destroyed fair\r\nmansions. The guards came out against him and he\r\nslew many of them.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2325",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie mengisahkan Indrajit membelenggu Hanuman dengan anak panah sihir berwujud ular.",
        "en": "Mackenzie recounts Indrajit binding Hanuman with a magical serpent arrow."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Ia mencoba membunuh Krishna dengan menyusui menggunakan susu beracun. Krishna mengisap susu sekaligus nyawanya dari payudaranya. Teks Bhagavata versi Vedabase menyatakan Putana memperoleh kedudukan ibu di dunia transendental setelah kematiannya.",
      "en": "She tries to kill Krishna by nursing him with poisoned milk. Krishna sucks both milk and life from her breasts.       "id": "vibhisana-c02",
      "source_id": "vibhisana-s1",
      "quote": "Vibhishana (Sanskrit: विभीषण, romanized: Vibhīṣaṇa, lit. 'formidable') is the younger brother of Ravana, the King of Lanka, in the ancient Indian epic Ramayana, and one of the eight Chiranjivis. Though a rakshasa himself, Vibhishana turned his back on Ravana, and defected to Rama's side, owing to his dharma. After Rama defeated Ravana",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Meski rakshasa, ia meninggalkan Ravana dan bergabung dengan Rama demi dharma.",
        "en": "Although a rakshasa he leaves Ravana and joins Rama for dharma."
      }
    },

      "id": "vibhisana-c03",
      "source_id": "vibhisana-s1",
      "quote": "Prince Vibhishana is portrayed as a pious and pure of heart in the epic. Pleased by his righteousness Brahma gave the boon of immortality.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Brahma memberinya keabadian setelah ia meminta pikirannya selalu mengikuti kebenaran.",
        "en": "Brahma grants him immortality after he asks for his mind always to follow righteousness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vibhisana-c04",
      "source_id": "vibhisana-s1",
      "quote": "Vibhishana was the youngest son of the rakshasi Kaikesi and the sage Vishrava, who was himself a son of the sage Pulastya, one of the Prajapati. Vibhishana was the younger brother of the King of Lanka, Ravana, and also the sibling of Kumbhakarna. Even though he was born as a rakshasa, he was pious and virtuous, upholding the values of dharma.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vibhisana-c05",
      "source_id": "vibhisana-s1",
      "quote": "Rama accepted Vibhishana's fealty and anointed him the King of Lanka after Ravana's death.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      "id": "vibhisana-c06",
      "source_id": "vibhisana-s2",

      "id": "Rigveda menggambarkan mereka memakai helm dan pelindung dada emas. Kapak mereka membelah awan sehingga hujan turun dan mereka dapat mengguncang gunung.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "maruts-c01",
      "source_id": "maruts-s1",
      "quote": "are storm deities and sons of Rudra and Prisni.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Marut adalah kelompok dewa badai, putra Rudra dan Prisni, dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "The Maruts are a group of Hindu storm deities, sons of Rudra and Prisni."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruts-c02",
      "source_id": "maruts-s1",
      "quote": "In the Vedic mythology, the Maruts act as Indra's companions as a troop of young warriors. According to French comparative mythologist Georges Dumézil, they are cognate to the Einherjar and the Wild hunt.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Weda mereka mendampingi Indra sebagai pasukan kesatria muda.",
        "en": "In Vedic mythology they accompany Indra as a troop of young warriors."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruts-c03",
      "source_id": "maruts-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Rig Veda they wore golden helmets and breastplates, and used their axes to split the clouds so that rain may fall. They were capable of causing the mountains to tremble. Though they were the offspring of Rudra, they were previously considered by Indra, The main chieftain of heaven, who was entitled as Marutvant (\"Accompanied by the Maruts\").",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rigveda menggambarkan mereka memakai helm dan pelindung dada emas.",
        "en": "The Rigveda depicts them wearing golden helmets and breastplates."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruts-c04",
      "source_id": "maruts-s1",
      "quote": "used their axes to split the clouds so that rain may fall. They were capable of causing the mountains to tremble.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kapak mereka membelah awan sehingga hujan turun dan mereka dapat mengguncang gunung.",
        "en": "Their axes split clouds to release rain and they can shake mountains."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruts-c05",
      "source_id": "maruts-s1",
      "quote": "They are also accompanied by a female deity, Rodasi, who rides with them through the heavens. She is variously described as their mother and wife of Rudra",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rodasi menemani mereka melintasi langit; salah satu versi menyebutnya ibu mereka dan istri Rudra.",
        "en": "Rodasi accompanies them through the heavens; one version calls her their mother and Rudra's wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "maruts-c06",
      "source_id": "maruts-s2",
      "quote": "The Maruts, the sons of red Rudra, were the spirits\r\nof tempest and thunder. To each of their chariots were\r\nyoked two spotted deer and one swift-footed, never-wearying\r\nred deer as leader.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 136",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menggambarkan kereta Marut ditarik dua rusa tutul dan seorang pemimpin berwarna merah.",
        "en": "Mackenzie describes Marut chariots drawn by two spotted deer and a red leader."
      }
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ulupi-c01",
      "source_id": "ulupi-s1",
      "quote": "Ulupi (Ulūpī), also known as Uluchi and Ulupika, is a Naga princess mentioned in the Hindu epic Mahabharata. Ulupi is the daughter of the king Kauravya, and is the second wife of Arjuna. She also finds a mention in the Vishnu Purana and the Bhagavata Purana.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ulupi adalah putri naga dalam Mahabharata, putri Kauravya dan istri kedua Arjuna.",
        "en": "Ulupi is a naga princess in the Mahabharata, Kauravya's daughter and Arjuna's second wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulupi-c02",
      "source_id": "ulupi-s1",
      "quote": "Ulupi is said to have met and married Arjuna when he was in exile, and with whom she bore his son Iravan. She is also credited with redeeming Arjuna from the curse of the Vasus by restoring his life after he was slain in a battle by his son, Babhruvahana.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bertemu dan menikah dengan Arjuna saat pengasingannya dan melahirkan Iravan.",
        "en": "She meets and marries Arjuna during his exile and bears Iravan."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulupi-c03",
      "source_id": "ulupi-s1",
      "quote": "Ulupi is said to have met and married Arjuna when he was in exile, and with whom she bore his son Iravan. She is also credited with redeeming Arjuna from the curse of the Vasus by restoring his life after he was slain in a battle by his son, Babhruvahana.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menghidupkan kembali Arjuna setelah ia dibunuh Babhruvahana, sehingga membebaskannya dari kutukan Vasu.",
        "en": "She revives Arjuna after Babhruvahana kills him, redeeming him from the Vasus' curse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulupi-c04",
      "source_id": "ulupi-s1",
      "quote": "Little is said about Ulupi in the Mahabharata. Ulupi is known by numerous names in the Mahabharata—Bhujagātmajā, Bhujagendrakanyakā, Bhujagottamā Kauravī, Kauravyaduhitā, Kauravyakulanandinī, Pannaganandinī, Pannagasutā, Pannagātmajā, Pannageśvarakanyā, Pannagī, and Uragātmajā.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mahabharata menyebutnya dengan banyak nama, termasuk Kauravyaduhita dan Pannagi.",
        "en": "The Mahabharata names her in many ways, including Kauravyaduhita and Pannagi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulupi-c05",
      "source_id": "ulupi-s1",
      "quote": "Ulupi is described as a mythical form of a Nāgakanyā (Nāga princess), half-maiden and half-serpent.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai nagakanya, sebagian perempuan dan sebagian ular.",
        "en": "She is depicted as a nagakanya, part woman and part serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulupi-c06",
      "source_id": "ulupi-s2",
      "quote": "brave Iravat, whose\r\nmother was Ulupi, the serpent nymph, daughter of the\r\nking of the Nagas.",

        "id": "Mackenzie menyebut Ulupi sebagai ibu Iravat dan nimfa ular, putri raja naga.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Iravan adalah putra Arjuna dan putri naga Ulupi dalam Mahabharata. Ia menjadi dewa utama dalam kultus Kuttantavar dan tokoh penting dalam tradisi Draupadi.",
      "en": "Iravan is Arjuna and the naga princess Ulupi's son in the Mahabharata. He becomes the central deity in the Kuttantavar cult and an important figure in Draupadi traditions.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iravan-c01",
        "iravan-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mahabharata mengisahkan kematiannya pada hari kedelapan perang Kurukshetra. Tradisi India Selatan menghormati pengorbanannya kepada Kali demi kemenangan Pandava. Dalam tradisi Draupadi, Krishna mengizinkan kepala Iravan yang terpenggal menyaksikan seluruh perang. Mackenzie menyebut Iravat pemberani sebagai salah satu pahlawan muda pasukan Pandava.",
      "en": "The Mahabharata recounts his death on the eighth day of the Kurukshetra war. South Indian traditions honor his sacrifice to Kali for the Pandavas' victory. In Draupadi traditions Krishna allows Iravan's severed head to witness the whole war. Mackenzie calls brave Iravat one of the Pandava army's young heroes.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "iravan-c05",
        "iravan-c06"

      "source_id": "iravan-s1",
      "quote": "Iravan  also known as Iravat and Iravant, is a minor character from the Hindu epic Mahabharata. The son of Pandava prince Arjuna (one of the main heroes of the Mahabharata) and the Naga princess Ulupi, Iravan is the central deity of the cult of Kuttantavar (Kuttandavar) which is also the name commonly given to him in that tradition—and plays a major role in the sect of Draupadi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi dewa utama dalam kultus Kuttantavar dan tokoh penting dalam tradisi Draupadi.",
        "en": "He becomes the central deity in the Kuttantavar cult and an important figure in Draupadi traditions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iravan-c03",
      "source_id": "iravan-s1",
      "quote": "The Mahabharata portrays Iravan as dying a heroic death on the 8th day of the 18-day Kurukshetra War, the epic's main subject. However, the South Indian traditions have a supplementary practice of honouring Aravan's self-sacrifice to the goddess Kali to ensure her favour and the victory of the Pandavas in the war.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mahabharata mengisahkan kematiannya pada hari kedelapan perang Kurukshetra.",

      }
    },
