    {
      "id": "iravan-c05",
      "source_id": "iravan-s1",
      "quote": "Krishna allows Aravan to witness the entire duration of the Mahabharata war through the eyes of his severed head.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Draupadi, Krishna mengizinkan kepala Iravan yang terpenggal menyaksikan seluruh perang.",
        "en": "In Draupadi traditions Krishna allows Iravan's severed head to witness the whole war."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iravan-c06",
      "source_id": "iravan-s2",
      "quote": "Among the young heroes were Arjuna's two sons,\r\nthe noble and peerless Abhimanyu, whose mother was\r\nKrishna's fair sister Subhadra, and brave Iravat,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1596",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menyebut Iravat pemberani sebagai salah satu pahlawan muda pasukan Pandava.",
        "en": "Mackenzie calls brave Iravat one of the Pandava army's young heroes."
      }
    }
  ],

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Takshaka adalah raja naga dalam Hindu dan Buddha yang disebut Mahabharata. Ia disebut sahabat Indra dan dahulu tinggal di Kurukshetra serta hutan Khandava.",
      "en": "Takshaka is a naga king in Hinduism and Buddhism mentioned in the Mahabharata. He is called Indra's friend and formerly resides in Kurukshetra and Khandava forest.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taksaka-c01",
        "taksaka-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Srutasena disebut adiknya. Ashvasena adalah putranya; saat pembakaran Khandava, Takshaka sedang berada di Kurukshetra. Ia membunuh Parikshit dengan gigitan sebagai pelaksanaan kutukan seorang putra resi. Dalam terjemahan Ganguli, Takshaka menyuap Kashyapa agar tidak pergi menyembuhkan raja.",
      "en": "Srutasena is called his younger brother. Ashvasena is his son; when Khandava burns Takshaka is in Kurukshetra. He kills Parikshit by biting him in fulfillment of a sage's son's curse. In Ganguli's translation Takshaka bribes Kasyapa to stop him from healing the king.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taksaka-c03",
        "taksaka-c04",
        "taksaka-c05",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Takshaka adalah raja naga dalam Hindu dan Buddha yang disebut Mahabharata.",
        "en": "Takshaka is a naga king in Hinduism and Buddhism mentioned in the Mahabharata."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taksaka-c02",
      "source_id": "taksaka-s1",
      "quote": "Takshaka is mentioned as the friend of Indra, the king of gods, at (1-225,227,230). Takshaka, formerly dwelt in Kurukshetra and the forest of Khandava (modern-day Delhi) (1,3). Takshaka and Ashvasena were constant companions who lived in Kurukshetra on the banks of the Ikshumati (1,3).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut sahabat Indra dan dahulu tinggal di Kurukshetra serta hutan Khandava.",
        "en": "He is called Indra's friend and formerly resides in Kurukshetra and Khandava forest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taksaka-c03",
      "source_id": "taksaka-s1",
      "quote": "Srutasena, the younger brother of Takshaka,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Srutasena disebut adiknya.",
        "en": "Srutasena is called his younger brother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taksaka-c04",
      "source_id": "taksaka-s1",
      "quote": "At that time the Naga chief Takshaka was not there, having gone to Kurukshetra. But Ashvasena, the mighty son of Takshaka, was there.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ashvasena adalah putranya; saat pembakaran Khandava, Takshaka sedang berada di Kurukshetra.",
        "en": "Ashvasena is his son; when Khandava burns Takshaka is in Kurukshetra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taksaka-c05",
      "source_id": "taksaka-s1",
      "quote": "When Parikshit was cursed by a sage's son to die by a snake bite for insulting his father, Takshaka came to fulfil the curse. Takshaka did the deed by approaching in disguise (1,50) and biting Parikshit, the grandson of Arjuna and thus slaying him, while he was meditating on Lord Vishnu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membunuh Parikshit dengan gigitan sebagai pelaksanaan kutukan seorang putra resi.",
        "en": "He kills Parikshit by biting him in fulfillment of a sage's son's curse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taksaka-c06",
      "source_id": "taksaka-s2",
      "quote": "Takshaka replied, ‘O best of regenerate\r\n      ones, even I will give thee more than what thou expectest from that king.\r\n      Therefore do not go.’",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 358",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam terjemahan Ganguli, Takshaka menyuap Kashyapa agar tidak pergi menyembuhkan raja.",
        "en": "In Ganguli's translation Takshaka bribes Kasyapa to stop him from healing the king."
      }
    }
  ],

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan putih seperti salju dan sebagai raja kuda.",
        "en": "He is depicted as snowy white and king of horses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uchchaihshravas-c03",
      "source_id": "uchchaihshravas-s1",
      "quote": "In Hinduism, Uchchaihshravas (Sanskrit: उच्चैःश्रवस्, IAST: Uccaiḥśravas or Sanskrit: उच्चैःश्रवा:, IAST: Uccaiḥśravāḥ), (lit. 'long-ears' or 'neighing aloud') is a seven-headed flying horse, created during the churning of the ocean. It is considered the best of horses, as prototype and king of the horses.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut tunggangan Indra, tetapi juga kuda Bali raja asura.",
        "en": "He is called Indra's mount but also the horse of Bali, king of asuras."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uchchaihshravas-c04",
      "source_id": "uchchaihshravas-s1",
      "quote": "The Mahabharata also mentions a bet between sisters and wives of Kashyapa - Vinata and Kadru about the colour of Uchchaihshravas's tail. While Vinata—the mother of Garuda and Aruna—said it was white, Kadru said it was black. The loser would have to become a servant of the winner.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Warna ekornya menjadi pokok taruhan Vinata dan Kadru; para putra ular Kadru membuat ekor tampak hitam.",
        "en": "His tail's color is the subject of Vinata and Kadru's wager; Kadru's serpent sons make the tail appear black."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uchchaihshravas-c05",
      "source_id": "uchchaihshravas-s1",
      "quote": "Uchchaihshravas is also mentioned in the Bhagavad Gita (10.27, which is part of the Mahabharata), a discourse by god Krishna to Arjuna. When Krishna declares himself to be the source of the universe, he declares that among horses, he is Uchchaihshravas—he who is born from the amrita.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bhagavad Gita 10.27 menyebutnya sebagai kuda yang lahir dari amrita.",
        "en": "Bhagavad Gita 10.27 mentions him as the horse born from amrita."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "uchchaihshravas-c06",
      "source_id": "uchchaihshravas-s2",
      "quote": "Divine, graceful, perpetually young, creation’s\r\n      master-piece, and of irresistible vigour, it was blest with every\r\n      auspicious mark.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 207",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Mahabharata Ganguli menyebut Uchchaihsravas kuda ilahi yang selalu muda.",
        "en": "Ganguli's Mahabharata translation calls Uchchaihsravas a divine steed that is perpetually young."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Trijata adalah rakshasi Ramayana yang ditugaskan menjaga Sita di Lanka. Dalam adaptasi belakangan ia disebut putri Vibhishana.",
      "en": "Trijata is a Ramayana rakshasi assigned to guard Sita in Lanka. In later adaptations she is called Vibhishana's daughter.",

        "trijata-c01",
        "trijata-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia digambarkan tua dan bijaksana serta bermimpi tentang kehancuran Ravana dan kemenangan Rama. Biasanya ia menjadi sahabat setia yang menghibur Sita dalam kesulitan. Namanya dalam versi regional termasuk Punukay di Laos dan Benyakai dalam Ramakien Thailand. Dalam terjemahan Griffith, Trijata meminta rakshasi lain menyelamatkan istri pewaris Dasharatha.",
      "en": "She is depicted as old and wise and dreams of Ravana's destruction and Rama's victory. She generally becomes a loyal friend comforting Sita in adversity.     {
      "id": "trijata-c02",
      "source_id": "trijata-s1",
      "quote": "Trijata (Sanskrit: त्रिजटा, IAST: Trijaṭā) is a rakshasi (demoness) in the Hindu epic Ramayana who is assigned the duty of guarding goddess Sita who was kidnapped by the demon-king of Lanka Ravana. In later adaptions of Ramayana, Trijata is described as a daughter of Vibhishana, the brother of Ravana.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam adaptasi belakangan ia disebut putri Vibhishana.",
        "en": "In later adaptations she is called Vibhishana's daughter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trijata-c03",
      "source_id": "trijata-s1",
      "quote": "Trijata appears as a wise old rakshasi, who dreams of Ravana's destruction and the victory of God Rama,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan tua dan bijaksana serta bermimpi tentang kehancuran Ravana dan kemenangan Rama.",
        "en": "She is depicted as old and wise and dreams of Ravana's destruction and Rama's victory."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trijata-c04",
      "source_id": "trijata-s1",
      "quote": "Barring a few exceptions where Trijata is cast as Ravana's agent, she is generally portrayed as a friend and loyal companion of Goddess Sita in Her adversity. On numerous occasions, she offers solace to Goddess Sita and brings news from the outside world; she also dissuades Goddess Sita from committing suicide.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Biasanya ia menjadi sahabat setia yang menghibur Sita dalam kesulitan.",
        "en": "She generally becomes a loyal friend comforting Sita in adversity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trijata-c05",
      "source_id": "trijata-s1",
      "quote": "While Indian, Javanese and Balinese versions of the Ramayana call her Trijata, she is known as Punukay in the Laotian Phra Lak Phra Lam, Benyakai (เบญกาย) in the Thai Ramakien and Devi Seri Jali in the Malay Hikayat Seri Rama.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dalam versi regional termasuk Punukay di Laos dan Benyakai dalam Ramakien Thailand.",
        "en": "Regional names include Punukay in Laos and Benyakai in Thailand's Ramakien."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trijata-c06",
      "source_id": "trijata-s2",
      "quote": "But Trijaṭá, of softer mould, A Rákshas matron wise and old, With pity for the captive moved, In words like these the fiends reproved: “Me, me,” she cried, “eat me, but spare The spouse of Daśaratha's heir.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1641",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam terjemahan Griffith, Trijata meminta rakshasi lain menyelamatkan istri pewaris Dasharatha.",
        "en": "In Griffith's translation Trijata asks the other rakshasis to spare Dasharatha's heir's wife."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Vetalas are a class of Hindu supernatural beings inhabiting charnel grounds.",
    "claim_ids": [

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vetala adalah golongan makhluk gaib Hindu yang menghuni tempat pembakaran jenazah. Roh ini menggunakan jasad yang dihidupkan kembali sebagai kendaraan dan dapat masuk atau meninggalkannya.",
      "en": "Vetalas are a class of Hindu supernatural beings inhabiting charnel grounds. These spirits use reanimated corpses as vehicles and can possess or leave them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vetala-c01",
        "vetala-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tradisi menggambarkannya mengganggu manusia, tetapi juga menjaga desa. Mantra dapat mengusirnya dan upacara pemakaman dapat membebaskannya dari keberadaan sebagai roh. Vetala Panchavimshati memuat dua puluh lima cerita dengan vetala dan raja Vikramaditya sebagai tokoh utama. Pengantar Burton menyebut Baital sebagai roh yang menghidupkan jasad mati.",
      "en": "Traditions depict them troubling people but also guarding villages. Mantras can repel them and funerary rites release them from ghostly existence. The Vetala Panchavimshati contains twenty five stories with a vetala and King Vikramaditya as central figures. Burton's introduction calls the Baital a spirit animating dead bodies.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "vetala-c05",
        "vetala-c06"
      ]

      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/files/2400/2400-h/2400-h.htm",
      "title": "Vikram and the Vampire, by Sir Richard F. Burton",
      "author": "Richard F. Burton",
      "publisher": "Longmans, Green; Project Gutenberg",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "folklore-collection",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vetala-c01",
      "source_id": "vetala-s1",
      "quote": "is a class of beings in Hindu mythology. They are usually defined as a knowledgeable (fortune telling) paranormal entity said to be dwelling at charnel grounds.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vetala adalah golongan makhluk gaib Hindu yang menghuni tempat pembakaran jenazah.",
        "en": "Vetalas are a class of Hindu supernatural beings inhabiting charnel grounds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vetala-c02",
      "source_id": "vetala-s1",
      "quote": "A vetala (Sanskrit: वेताल, romanized: vetāla) is a class of beings in Hindu mythology. They are usually defined as a knowledgeable (fortune telling) paranormal entity said to be dwelling at charnel grounds. Reanimated corpses are used as vehicles by these spirits for movement. A vetala may possess and leave a dead body at will.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh ini menggunakan jasad yang dihidupkan kembali sebagai kendaraan dan dapat masuk atau meninggalkannya.",
        "en": "These spirits use reanimated corpses as vehicles and can possess or leave them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vetala-c03",
      "source_id": "vetala-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu folklore, the vetala is an evil spirit who haunts cemeteries and takes demonic possession of corpses. They make their displeasure known by troubling humans. They can drive people mad, kill children, and cause miscarriages, but also guard villages.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi menggambarkannya mengganggu manusia, tetapi juga menjaga desa.",
        "en": "Traditions depict them troubling people but also guarding villages."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vetala-c04",
      "source_id": "vetala-s1",
      "quote": "These creatures can be repelled by the chanting of mantras. One can free them from their ghostly existence by performing their funerary rites.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mantra dapat mengusirnya dan upacara pemakaman dapat membebaskannya dari keberadaan sebagai roh.",
        "en": "Mantras can repel them and funerary rites release them from ghostly existence."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vetala-c05",
      "source_id": "vetala-s1",
      "quote": "The Vetala Panchavimshati is a collection of twenty-five stories, featuring a vetala as one of the two central characters. It is a part of the Kathasaritsagara, a work that was compiled no later than the 11th century. It features a frame story of a sorcerer who asks King Vikramaditya to capture a corpse hanging on a tree that stands in the middle of a cremation ground.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vetala Panchavimshati memuat dua puluh lima cerita dengan vetala dan raja Vikramaditya sebagai tokoh utama.",
        "en": "The Vetala Panchavimshati contains twenty five stories with a vetala and King Vikramaditya as central figures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vetala-c06",
      "source_id": "vetala-s2",
      "quote": "a Vampire or evil\r\n      spirit which animates dead bodies",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 33",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pengantar Burton menyebut Baital sebagai roh yang menghidupkan jasad mati.",
        "en": "Burton's introduction calls the Baital a spirit animating dead bodies."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    {
      "id": "Sharabha adalah makhluk berkaki delapan yang sebagian singa dan sebagian burung dalam Hindu. Sastra Sanskerta menggambarkannya mampu melompati lembah dalam sekali lompatan.",
      "en": "Sharabha is an eight legged being, part lion and part bird, in Hinduism. Sanskrit literature describes him clearing a valley in one jump.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sharabha-c01",
        "sharabha-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam teks Shaiva, Shiva mengambil wujud Sharabha untuk menenangkan Narasimha. Sejumlah versi Vaishnava menyebut Gandabherunda mengalahkan Sharabha. Dalam kisah Jataka, Sharabha merupakan kelahiran terdahulu Buddha. Rubin menyimpan fragmen bingkai takhta berjudul Sharabha dari Nepal atau Tibet, abad keempat belas hingga kelima belas.",
      "en": "In Shaiva texts Shiva takes Sharabha's form to pacify Narasimha. Some Vaishnava versions say Gandabherunda defeats Sharabha. In Jataka narratives Sharabha is a former birth of the Buddha. The Rubin holds a throne frame fragment titled Sharabha from Nepal or Tibet, dated to the fourteenth to fifteenth century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sharabha-c03",
        "sharabha-c04",
        "sharabha-c05",

    {

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Sanskrit literature describes him clearing a valley in one jump."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sharabha-c03",
      "source_id": "sharabha-s1",
      "quote": "The Shaiva scriptures narrate that the deity Shiva assumed the form of Sharabha to pacify Narasimha - the fierce man-lion avatar of Vishnu worshipped by the Vaishnava sect. This form is popularly known as Sharabheshvara (\"Lord Sharabha\") or Sharabheshvaramurti.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sharabha-c04",
      "source_id": "sharabha-s1",
      "quote": "Vishnu assumed the form of the ferocious two-headed bird Gandabherunda, who in turn defeated Sharabha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejumlah versi Vaishnava menyebut Gandabherunda mengalahkan Sharabha.",
        "en": "Some Vaishnava versions say Gandabherunda defeats Sharabha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sharabha-c05",
      "source_id": "sharabha-s1",
      "quote": "In Buddhism, Sharabha appears in Jataka Tales as an earlier birth of the Buddha. It also appears in Tibetan Buddhist art, symbolizing the perfection of effort. As a figure of power and majesty, Sharabha has appeared in numerous emblems.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah Jataka, Sharabha merupakan kelahiran terdahulu Buddha.",
        "en": "In Jataka narratives Sharabha is a former birth of the Buddha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sharabha-c06",
      "source_id": "sharabha-s2",
      "quote": "Sharabha; fragment of a throne frame Explore Artwork Nepal or Tibet 14th - 15th century",

      "id": "Tataka adalah tokoh yakshi antagonis dalam Ramayana yang mengganggu ritual para resi bersama Maricha. Suketu memperoleh Tataka sebagai putri dengan kekuatan seribu gajah.",
      "en": "Tataka is an antagonistic yakshi in the Ramayana troubling sages' rituals with Maricha. Suketu receives Tataka as a daughter with the strength of a thousand elephants.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tataka-c01",
        "tataka-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia menikah dengan Sunda dan melahirkan Maricha. Agastya mengutuknya menjadi pemakan manusia setelah ia dan Maricha menyerangnya. Rama membunuhnya dengan satu panah ke dada atas arahan Vishvamitra. Dalam terjemahan Griffith, Tataka disebut sebagai putri Suketu yang dianugerahkan setelah tapa.",
      "en": "She marries Sunda and bears Maricha. Agastya curses her to become a man eater after she and Maricha attack him. Rama kills her with one arrow to the chest under Vishvamitra's direction. In Griffith's translation Tataka is called Suketu's daughter granted after austerities.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tataka-c01",
      "source_id": "tataka-s1",
      "quote": "Tāṭakā is a minor yakṣī antagonist in the Rāmāyaṇa. Along with her son, Mārīca, Tāṭakā would harass and attack sages performing yajñas in the forest.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tataka adalah tokoh yakshi antagonis dalam Ramayana yang mengganggu ritual para resi bersama Maricha.",
        "en": "Tataka is an antagonistic yakshi in the Ramayana troubling sages' rituals with Maricha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tataka-c02",
      "source_id": "tataka-s1",
      "quote": "In the Rāmāyaṇa, Viśvāmitra tells Rāma and Lakṣmaṇa the story of Tāṭakā when they reach a forest inhabited by her. Viśvāmitra states that a yakṣa named Suketu had undertaken austerities to obtain children, and was given a daughter with the strength of a thousand elephants named Tāṭakā, but not a son. When she became of age, she was married to Sunda, and gave birth to a son named Mārīca.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suketu memperoleh Tataka sebagai putri dengan kekuatan seribu gajah.",
        "en": "Suketu receives Tataka as a daughter with the strength of a thousand elephants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tataka-c03",
      "source_id": "tataka-s1",
      "quote": "she was married to Sunda, and gave birth to a son named Mārīca.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menikah dengan Sunda dan melahirkan Maricha.",
        "en": "She marries Sunda and bears Maricha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tataka-c04",
      "source_id": "tataka-s1",
      "quote": "Tāṭakā and Mārīca attack the seer Agastya, who curses Mārīca to become a rākṣasa and curses Tāṭakā to become an ugly man-eater.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Agastya mengutuknya menjadi pemakan manusia setelah ia dan Maricha menyerangnya.",
        "en": "Agastya curses her to become a man eater after she and Maricha attack him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tataka-c05",
      "source_id": "tataka-s1",
      "quote": "Tāṭakā then charges Rāma, who proceeds to shoot and kill her with a single arrow to the chest.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rama membunuhnya dengan satu panah ke dada atas arahan Vishvamitra.",
        "en": "Rama kills her with one arrow to the chest under Vishvamitra's direction."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tataka-c06",
      "source_id": "tataka-s2",
      "quote": "A mighty spirit lived of yore; Suketu was the name he bore. Childless was he, and free from crime In rites austere he passed his time. The mighty Sire was pleased to show His favour, and a child bestow. Táḍaká named, most fair to see,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1641",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam terjemahan Griffith, Tataka disebut sebagai putri Suketu yang dianugerahkan setelah tapa.",
        "en": "In Griffith's translation Tataka is called Suketu's daughter granted after austerities."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Danava adalah golongan keturunan Kashyapa dan Danu, putri Daksha, dalam keyakinan Hindu. Mereka merupakan bagian dari asura dan saudara seayah para deva serta daitya.",
      "en": "Danavas are a class descending from Kashyapa and Danu, Daksha's daughter, in Hindu belief. They belong to the asuras and are half brothers of devas and daityas.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka biasanya digambarkan melawan dewa, tetapi hubungan dan batas identitasnya beragam dalam sejarah tradisi. Nama Danava berasal dari nama ibu mereka, Danu. Menurut kisah yang diringkas, para deva mengusir mereka dari Svarga pada Satya Yuga dan mereka berlindung di Vindhya. Retelling Mackenzie menempatkan Danava dan Daitya di Patala, di bawah kuasa Varuna.",
      "en": "They are usually depicted opposing deities but their relationships and identity boundaries vary within the tradition's history. The name Danava comes from their mother Danu's name.       "id": "danava-hinduism-c01",
      "source_id": "danava-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu belief, the danavas are a race descending from Kashyapa and his wife Danu, a daughter of the progenitor god, Daksha. It is mentioned that there are one hundred danavas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Danava adalah golongan keturunan Kashyapa dan Danu, putri Daksha, dalam keyakinan Hindu.",
        "en": "Danavas are a class descending from Kashyapa and Danu, Daksha's daughter, in Hindu belief."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danava-hinduism-c02",
      "source_id": "danava-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "The danavas are a legendary race of asuras, the half-brothers to the devas and daityas that are found in a range of Hindu texts. The danavas are a part of a larger group of the asuras, and are typically portrayed as opposed to the Hindu deities.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka merupakan bagian dari asura dan saudara seayah para deva serta daitya.",
        "en": "They belong to the asuras and are half brothers of devas and daityas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danava-hinduism-c03",
      "source_id": "danava-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "The danavas are a legendary race of asuras, the half-brothers to the devas and daityas that are found in a range of Hindu texts. The danavas are a part of a larger group of the asuras, and are typically portrayed as opposed to the Hindu deities.",

      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "danava-hinduism-c04",
      "source_id": "danava-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "The name danavas stems from the mother's name: Danu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Danava berasal dari nama ibu mereka, Danu.",

    {
      "id": "danava-hinduism-c05",
      "source_id": "danava-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "The devas exiled the danavas from Svarga during the Satya Yuga. After the exile, the danavas are held to have taken refuge in the Vindhya range.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

    {

      "id": "Vidyadhara adalah golongan makhluk gaib agama India yang memiliki kekuatan sihir. Dalam Hindu mereka mengiringi Shiva dan digolongkan sebagai setengah dewa.",
      "en": "Vidyadharas are a class of Indian religious supernatural beings possessing magical powers. In Hinduism they attend Shiva and are considered demigods.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vidyadhara-c01",
        "vidyadhara-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Epos Hindu menggambarkan mereka sebagai roh udara yang menabur bunga dan bergembira dengan musik. Mereka memiliki kekuatan untuk mengecilkan ukuran tubuh. Narasi Jain menggambarkan mereka sebagai garis keturunan manusia atau semi-ilahi dengan ilmu atau kekuatan vidya. Catatan terjemahan Ramayana Griffith menempatkan Vidyadhara bersama Siddha di udara antara bumi dan matahari.",
      "en": "Hindu epics depict them as air spirits scattering flowers and rejoicing with music. They have powers to diminish their size. Jain narratives describe them as human or semidivine lineages with vidya sciences or powers. Griffith's Ramayana translation note places Vidyadharas with Siddhas in the air between earth and sun.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vidyadhara-c02",
      "source_id": "vidyadhara-s1",
      "quote": "In Hinduism, they also attend Shiva, who lives in the Himalayas. They are considered Upadevas, or demi-gods.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "vidyadhara-c03",
      "source_id": "vidyadhara-s1",
      "quote": "Vidyādharas are described as essentially spirits of the air. ... strewing flowers watching a combat, rejoicing with music and laughter,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Epos Hindu menggambarkan mereka sebagai roh udara yang menabur bunga dan bergembira dengan musik.",

      }
    },

      "id": "vidyadhara-c04",
      "source_id": "vidyadhara-s1",
      "quote": "They possess great magical powers like the ability to diminish their size.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "They have powers to diminish their size."

      "id": "vidyadhara-c05",
      "source_id": "vidyadhara-s1",
      "quote": "Jain narrative texts describe Vidyadharas as human or semi-divine lineages who possess Vidyas, or special sciences and powers. In Jain versions of the Ramayana, the Vanaras and Rakshasas are not treated as literal monkeys and demons.       "source_id": "yali-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Yali (IAST: Yāḷi, Tamil: யாளி), also called Vyāla (Sanskrit: व्याल), is a Hindu mythological creature, portrayed with the head and the body of a lion, the trunk and the tusks of an elephant, and sometimes bearing equine features.       "source_id": "yali-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Descriptions of, and references to, yalis are ancient, but they became prominent in South Indian sculptures in the 16th century. Yalis were described to be more powerful than the lion, the tiger, or the elephant.     "en": "Kirtimukha is a fierce face with large fangs and a gaping mouth in Hindu and Buddhist architecture.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kirtimukha-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kirtimukha adalah wajah ganas bertaring besar dan bermulut menganga dalam arsitektur Hindu dan Buddha. Motif hias ini memiliki asal kisah dalam Skanda Purana dan Shiva Purana.",
      "en": "Kirtimukha is a fierce face with large fangs and a gaping mouth in Hindu and Buddhist architecture. This ornamental motif has a narrative origin in the Skanda Purana and Shiva Purana.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kirtimukha-c01",
        "kirtimukha-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mukha berarti wajah dan kirti berarti kemasyhuran atau kemuliaan. Zimmer menafsirkannya sebagai topeng demon penolak bahaya dan penjaga ambang. Motif ini menghias lintel pintu ruang suci, sering hanya memperlihatkan rahang atas dan bagian atas wajah. NHB Singapura menyebut kirtimukha sebagai wajah kemuliaan yang mujur dalam arsitektur kuil Hindu-Buddha Asia Selatan dan Tenggara.",
      "en": "Mukha means face and kirti means fame or glory. Zimmer interprets it as an apotropaic demon mask and guardian of the threshold. The motif decorates sanctum doorway lintels, often showing only the upper jaw and upper face. Singapore's NHB calls kirtimukha an auspicious face of glory in South and Southeast Asian Hindu Buddhist temple architecture.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kirtimukha-c03",
        "kirtimukha-c04",
        "kirtimukha-c05",
        "kirtimukha-c06"
      ]

  "claims": [

      "id": "kirtimukha-c01",
      "source_id": "kirtimukha-s1",
      "quote": "is the name of a swallowing fierce face with huge fangs, and gaping mouth, very common in the iconography of Hindu temple architecture in South Asia and Southeast Asia, and often also found in Buddhist architecture.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kirtimukha adalah wajah ganas bertaring besar dan bermulut menganga dalam arsitektur Hindu dan Buddha.",
        "en": "Kirtimukha is a fierce face with large fangs and a gaping mouth in Hindu and Buddhist architecture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kirtimukha-c02",
      "source_id": "kirtimukha-s1",
      "quote": "Unlike other Hindu legendary creatures, for example the sea-dwelling makara, the kirtimukha is essentially an ornamental motif in art, which has its origin in a legend from the Skanda Purana and the Shiva Purana.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Motif hias ini memiliki asal kisah dalam Skanda Purana dan Shiva Purana.",
        "en": "This ornamental motif has a narrative origin in the Skanda Purana and Shiva Purana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kirtimukha-c03",
      "source_id": "kirtimukha-s1",
      "quote": "The word mukha in Sanskrit refers to the face while kīrti means \"fame, glory\".  The story of Kirtimukha begins when the asura king Jalandhara, who \"by virtue of extraordinary austerities ... accumulated to himself irresistible powers.\" In a burst of pride, he sent forth his messenger, the Hindu deity Rahu, whose main task is eclipsing the moon, to challenge Shiva. \"The challenge ...",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mukha berarti wajah dan kirti berarti kemasyhuran atau kemuliaan.",
        "en": "Mukha means face and kirti means fame or glory."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kirtimukha-c04",
      "source_id": "kirtimukha-s1",
      "quote": "The Kirtimukha is often used as a motif surmounting the pinnacle of a temple or the image of a deity, especially in South Indian architecture. As Zimmer writes, \"Kirtimukha serves primarily as an apotropaic demon-mask, a gruesome, awe-inspiring guardian of the threshold.\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zimmer menafsirkannya sebagai topeng demon penolak bahaya dan penjaga ambang.",
        "en": "Zimmer interprets it as an apotropaic demon mask and guardian of the threshold."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kirtimukha-c05",
      "source_id": "kirtimukha-s1",
      "quote": "This fierce face with bulging eyes sits also as an embellishment over the lintel of the gate to the inner sanctum in many Hindu temples ... very often only the upper jaw and top of the face is visible",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Motif ini menghias lintel pintu ruang suci, sering hanya memperlihatkan rahang atas dan bagian atas wajah.",
        "en": "The motif decorates sanctum doorway lintels, often showing only the upper jaw and upper face."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kirtimukha-c06",
      "source_id": "kirtimukha-s2",
      "quote": "The kirtimukha or auspicious face of glory is popular icon in South and Southeast Asian Hindu-Buddhist temple architecture.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "NHB Singapura menyebut kirtimukha sebagai wajah kemuliaan yang mujur dalam arsitektur kuil Hindu-Buddha Asia Selatan dan Tenggara.",
        "en": "Singapore's NHB calls kirtimukha an auspicious face of glory in South and Southeast Asian Hindu Buddhist temple architecture."
      }
    }
  ],

        "en": "In the legend Narasimha takes this form to subdue Sharabha, Shiva's form."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gandabherunda-c03",
      "source_id": "gandabherunda-s1",
      "quote": "Gandabherunda (IAST: Gaṇḍabheruṇḍa) is a form of Vishnu in Hindu mythology. According to legend, Narasimha, an avatar of Vishnu, assumes this form of a double-headed eagle to subdue Sharabha, a form of Shiva.  Gandabherunda is worshipped along with his consort Narasimhi, a fierce form of Lakshmi, also revered as Simhamukhi Lakshmi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipuja bersama Narasimhi, wujud ganas Lakshmi.",
        "en": "He is worshipped with Narasimhi, a fierce form of Lakshmi."

      "source_id": "gandabherunda-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "gandabherunda-c05",
      "source_id": "gandabherunda-s1",
      "quote": "In the Chennakeshava Temple, Beluru, Karnataka, Gandabherunda as a two headed eagle is carved in a scene of chain of destruction, which results in the destruction of the universe.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

      "quote": "the Gandabherunda, a mythic double headed bird, the royal insignia of the Wodeyar rulers (1399 -1947) of Mysore.",

      "source_id": "kaliya-s1",
      "quote": "The proper home of Kāliya was the island of Ramaṇaka, but he had been driven away from there in fear of Garuḍa, the foe of all serpents.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
